# EveryAIOS — Complete Codebase Map (HLD + LLD)

> **Generated by:** `node scripts/gen-codebase-map.mjs` (regenerates §9–§15; the narrative in §1–§8 is hand-authored).
> **Repository:** `sarv-projects/EveryAIOS` · branch `main` · 1,416 tracked files / 311 commits.
> **Toolchain in use:** node 22.23.1 · cargo 1.98.0 · bun 1.3.14 · pnpm 11.9.0.

---

## 0. How to read this map (coverage & confidence)

This repository is **395,551 lines across 1,410 text files** (1,416 tracked files in total, counted at
generation time — §15.4). A literal per-function prose description of every one of them is not a
document, it is a second implementation. This map therefore uses **four coverage
tiers**, and every statement is tagged with the tier it came from so you know how much to trust it:

| Tier | What it means | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|
| **[V]** **Verified** | I read the code or ran the command. | §1–§8, and every count in §9–§15 (computed from `git ls-files` / the files on disk at generation time) |
| **[L]** **Logic-level** | Function-by-function behaviour, including failure behaviour. | §2 (the 16 core subsystems) |
| **[S]** **Signature-level** | Every public item enumerated, with its `//!` / `///` doc sentence. No prose. | §9, §10, and the parsed manifest/config facts in §13 |
| **[A]** **Accounted** | Every function *name* (public **and private**) listed with its line number, plus the file's line count. Nothing is omitted; nothing is explained. | §9, §10; every remaining file's purpose in §13, §14 |
| **[D]** **Derived** | Mechanical inference (wiring reachability, reference counts). Heuristics — see the caveats in §11. | §11, and the script wiring verdicts in §13.7 |
| **[C]** **Claimed** | Asserted by the repo's own docs (`TODO.md`, `CURRENT_RUN.md`, `SPEC-CHANGELOG.md`) and **not** independently verified here. | §8 |
| **[E]** **Enforced** | Not a claim: the generator diffs its own output against `git ls-files` and **exits non-zero** if any tracked file lacks an entry. | §15.5 (the coverage audit) |

**The rule for §8:** anything marked **[C]** should be re-verified before you rely on it. This repo's
own handover (`CURRENT_RUN.md`) explicitly warns that older entries are superseded and that its
census must not be treated as authority over executed evidence.

---

## 1. High-level design (HLD)

### 1.1 What EveryAIOS is

EveryAIOS is a **local-first, bring-your-own-key desktop harness**. It is not a coding agent; it is the
operating layer that *hosts* coding agents (Claude Code, OpenAI Codex, OpenCode, Grok Build) and
general models, and supplies what those agents lack: durable git worktrees, a cryptographic mutation
gate, an encrypted key vault, native Office primitives, a tiered browser, OS-level computer use,
cognitive memory, and a tamper-evident audit log. The product tagline in its own docs is the
**"Universal Agentic OS & Desktop Harness."**

Two design decisions dominate everything else in the code:

1. **Hybrid & decoupled.** The TypeScript engine (`@everyaios/core-*`, vendored under `packages/`)
   stays as a supervised Bun sidecar. Rust owns only the paths where native code genuinely wins:
   sandboxing, browser/CDP, spreadsheet recalculation, storage intelligence, audit ingest, memory graph.
2. **The sidecar proposes; the Rust core disposes.** Every mutating effect requires an authorization
   ticket minted in Rust. This is the single most important invariant in the repository, and it is
   enforced structurally rather than by convention (see §1.3).

### 1.2 Topology (the four layers)

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ L4  COCKPIT           ui/ — React 19 + Zustand 5 + Tailwind 4                 │
│     ui/src/App.tsx (one shell, no peer tabs)                                 │
│     store.ts (3,019 lines) = the client state machine                        │
└───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │ Tauri IPC: invoke("<cmd>", args) + event listeners
                │ 349 registered commands, ONE generate_handler! macro
┌───────────────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ L3  SHELL             src-tauri/ — Tauri v2, deliberately thin               │
│     47 files, 40 of them *_cmds.rs command families                          │
│     lib.rs wires ChatRelay + terminal plane + desktop backend at boot        │
└───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │ direct Rust calls (path deps, not IPC)
┌───────────────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ L2  KERNEL            crates/ — 22 crates, 504 .rs files                     │
│     guard · vault · audit · office · browser · cdp · storage · memory ·      │
│     codeintel · acp · mcp · blueprint · core (orchestrator, 75 modules)      │
└───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │ stdio JSON-RPC 2.0
                │ frame = [u32 LE length][JSON payload]   (everyaios-ipc)
                │ protocol version = 1 on BOTH sides
┌───────────────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ L1  SIDECAR           packages/coordinator — Bun binary (bun --compile)      │
│     index.ts = frame loop + handshake; chat.ts (1,558 lines) = turn loop     │
└───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                │ out-of-process, decoupled
    ┌───────────┴──────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────┐
    │ ACP stdio            │ MCP stdio/SSE    │ CDP WebSocket    │
    ▼                      ▼                  ▼                  ▼
 Claude Code,           GitHub, Slack,    system Chrome/     external
 Codex, OpenCode,       Postgres, Linear  Electron apps      APIs
 Grok, 46-agent registry
```

**Process model.** `src-tauri` is the parent. `everyaios-core::supervisor::ProcessSupervisor` spawns the
compiled coordinator binary, monitors its exit code, applies exponential backoff, and trips a circuit
breaker on repeated crashes. The sidecar announces readiness with a `session/ready` notification as its
first byte on stdout (the supervisor's `Starting → Running` edge) and emits `session/heartbeat` every
10s so a 30s idle watchdog cannot false-kill a healthy-but-idle process. On stdin EOF the sidecar exits
0 — orphan prevention is a protocol property, not a cleanup job.

**Third topology.** L3–L1 above describe the desktop, which is the *control plane*. The same L2 binary
also runs as a headless executor node on hardware the user owns — the `deploy/` pack, documented in
§1.9 and inventoried in §13.3.

### 1.3 The trust split (one effect-authorization model)

This is the load-bearing invariant. Concretely:

- **Provider keys never cross the wire.** Rust's vault holds the key and there is no IPC route that
  returns one. Since `P71.2c`/`P71.2d` the sidecar makes **no model call of its own** — the retired
  `provider/stream` broker (`chat/provider_chunk` replies) is archived in `ARCH/archive/core-engine/`.
  The vault broker's remaining duty is to hand an agent process the credential material *it* needs
  (`EVERYAIOS_VAULT_*`, or a scoped endpoint for a model-only agent) and to observe, never to infer.
  What the desktop does keep is the **observation** half: catalogue/vault façades, pricing and usage
  ledgers fed by the agent's own reported turns.
- **Mutating tools need a ticket.** `everyaios-guard::ticket::AuthorizationTicket` is short-lived. Rust
  performs the pattern scan + path resolution + permission decision *first*, then issues the ticket; the
  sidecar's tool runtime rejects any unticketed mutation. Guard-2 (`ticket.rs`, `diffcard.rs`) adds the
  human-in-the-loop approval card for destructive/ask-classified operations.
- **The sidecar's own unguarded OS access is confined to its data dir.** Browser control, script-eval,
  OAuth-token use, and shell outside the granted workspace always execute in Rust regardless of ticket.

Authorization is one model for both actors: an **agent/automation** presents an `AuthorizationTicket`;
a **human UI gesture** is a trusted native gesture. Both write to the same append-only audit log, which
is why the `activity` screen and the Progress timeline can disclose rather than duplicate state.

### 1.4 The 8 full-stack modules → code

The repo's own organizing fiction (`ARCH/00-INDEX.md`, `ARCH/02-MODULE-LAYOUT.md`) is that every file
belongs to exactly one of eight "Full-Stack Modules". The mapping is real and worth preserving, because
it is how the docs cross-reference code:

| # | Module | Rust | TS | UI |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Universal harness & swarm | `acp`, `core/{multirun,worktrees,governor}`, `blueprint/{subagent,swarm}`, `agents` | `coordinator/{chief,router,fleet}` (the turn loop itself is the bound agent's — `coordinator/chat.ts` archived) | Agent picker, `diff` rail |
| 2 | Model gateway & vault | `vault`, `catalog` | `core-providers`, `core-ai` | Settings → Providers/Local Models, `guard.html` |
| 3 | Cockpit shell & compaction | `engine`, `ipc`, `src-tauri` | `coordinator/{budget,context-trace}` (`prompt` + `core-engine` archived, `P71.2c`) | `App.tsx`, shell/, 19 rails |
| 4 | Governed MCP & marketplace | `mcp` | `core-connectors` | `connectors` screen |
| 5 | Work-native primitives | `office`, `browser`, `cdp`, `desktop` | `coordinator/cua-*` | office-\*, `browse`, `desktop` rails |
| 6 | Durable work & memory | `memory`, `storage`, `codeintel`, `eval` | `core-memory` | `memory`/`projects`/`files`, `graph` rail |
| 7 | Automations & calendar | `core/{automation_runtime,scheduler_service}`, `src-tauri/scheduler_fire` | `coordinator/scheduler` (webhook ingress) | `automations`, `calendar` |
| 8 | Guard-2 & Merkle audit | `guard`, `audit`, `script` | `core-tools/{trust-ladder,permission-gate}` | `guard`, `activity` |

### 1.5 The six runtime data flows

**Flow 1 — a chat turn (the agent's, mediated).** `P71.2c` deleted the old path (`invoke("chat_stream")` →
`chat/stream` → `runChatStream` detached on the sidecar). What a v1 turn is now:

1. UI `sendMessage` resolves the **bound agent** — session pin → user default → selected agent. The retired
   built-in spellings resolve to *nothing*: an unbound session opens agent discovery and states the reason
   rather than substituting a second owner of model selection (`P71.6a`).
2. `acp_launch` brings that agent up on the ACP channel; the first turn also carries the P53.4
   compact-before-swap handoff bundle.
3. `acp_prompt` runs the turn behind the two gates the deleted dispatch used to own: **P71.3f readiness**
   (a non-`Ready` agent fails closed with the state named) and the **J11 session budget** pre-flight
   (`ChatRelay::preflight_session_budget`). It also opens an execution record and appends the P53.5 turn
   record. A budget refusal still surfaces as `code:"budget_exceeded"`, message `stopped: $X limit`.
4. The agent reasons with its **own** loop and calls the provider with its **own** transport. There is no
   `provider/stream` broker and no `chat/provider_chunk` reply: keys never cross the wire, so there is
   nothing to broker (`P71.2d`).
5. Output reaches the UI through the relay's `chat-event` emit (`chat/ttft` · `chat/batch` · `chat/done` ·
   `chat/error` · `chat/cancelled`) plus `acp_tool_log` / session updates; Pause and Stop call `acp_cancel`
   on the live handle.

**Flow 2 — a mutating tool call.** Two callers reach the same funnel. A **native/MCP façade call** (the
shared plane, `coordinator/src/mcp-bridge.ts` → `guard/evaluate`) and an **external agent's own tool call**
(ACP `session/request_permission` → the `FnMut` policy seam in `acp::client`) both land on `GuardService`,
which decides allow / ask / block. On `ask`, a ticket is minted and a card is rendered in the isolated
guard window; on approval the ticket is consumed → the effect executes → `audit` appends a hash-chained row.
An `Ask` **never auto-allows** — the ACP seam denies on reject/timeout.

**Flow 3 — a file edit.** The coordinator-side interceptor (`chat.ts` `editArgsFromToolCall` /
`applyExactEdit` / `deriveEditRisk`) was archived with the loop (`P71.2c`); the **Rust ladder is the applier
of record and the v1 path**. `file_ops.edit` validates fail-closed (absent/blank `path` or non-string
`old`/`new` throws *before* any read; `new:""` is a deletion, distinguished from absence) → the three-rung
ladder splices → `deriveEditRisk` infers structural risk from the splice itself (declaration-count delta
over `fn|class|struct|impl|trait|enum|interface|def|func|type`, or bracket-balance delta) →
`execution/preflight` with the staged candidate → on a failing verdict the edit is refused *before* any
mutating `tool/commit` → else `file_ops.write` commits exactly the bytes the gate inspected. `record_edit`
carries the rung that actually spliced. An external agent editing its own working tree does not pass
through this ladder — it is an external process the user launched, gated by the permission seam and audited
at the boundary, not instrumented per-write.

**Flow 4 — computer use (CUA).** The **Rust** CUA plane owns this (`everyaios-core::cua` + the desktop
backend `everyaios-desktop`), and a v1 caller reaches it through the shared plane (`desktop.*` tools →
`tool/exec`), not through a TS hook: a caller writes a five-part brief (`execution/cua_set_brief`) → the DAG
is persisted (`execution/cua_persist`) → `route_work_surface` decides *which* surface the work belongs on,
`refuse_cli_named_subagent` refuses a CLI-named subagent and office/URL targets are refused for `desktop.act`
→ a vision gate precedes any on-screen read (`screen_text_is_untrusted`) → Scout/Worker/Verifier roles run
with `filter_tools_for_role` → the Worker halts after two identical failures → a mechanical verifier checks
disk/command state and **ignores the Worker's own "done" claim** → `BLOCKED != FAILED` (a permission or
missing-information stop does not burn the fail budget) → fully verified traces can be promoted to a
`SKILL.md`.

The coordinator-side mirrors that prepared briefs from a turn (`cua-brief` · `cua-replan` · `cua-skill` ·
`cua-verify` · `cua-stop` · `cua-perceive` · `cua-route`) are **test-only after `P71.2c`** — their only
caller was the archived loop. They are declared, not deleted (`P71.2c` narrowing note).

**Flow 5 — an automation.** The **host** owns the tick: `scheduler_fire.rs` finds due firings and runs each
through the session's **bound agent** on the same ACP path an interactive turn takes, so an automation is
subject to the same guard gate; there is no privileged path (`ARCH/AUTOMATION.md` §9). The sidecar's role
shrank to the F11 loopback **webhook ingress** (`scheduler.ts` `startWebhookIngress` → `scheduler/fire_webhook`);
its due-ticker, chat-engine run and monitor verdict were removed with the built-in engine (`P71.2c`). A run
that cannot start is recorded with the reason named (not ready / unbound / budget), never a silent skip.

**Flow 6 — vault unlock.** `VaultGate` renders a locked shell → `vault_key_status` reports whether a
keyfile/passphrase gate is needed (`everyaios-core::vault_key`) → `vault_unlock` / `vault_setup` derive
the SQLCipher key → the vault opens and the **observation** sweep runs — the relay's connected-provider capability probe
(`catalog_cmds`), whose results every registry replays (A11, `ARCH/ROUTING.md` §6). Nothing is dialled. Wrong key must fail closed; this is covered by an acceptance
suite that also proves at-rest encryption and multi-session restart hydration.

### 1.6 Persistence & the data layer

All local databases use **SQLite in WAL mode** (`PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL; busy_timeout=5000`): readers
never block, the single writer is serialized by a Rust mutex, and per-agent write queues drain FIFO into
the writer. Distinct stores, deliberately kept separate:

| Store | Owner | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| Encrypted vault (SQLCipher/SQLite) | `everyaios-vault` | credentials, OAuth tokens, key pools, session budgets, `token_usage` ledger |
| Audit log (NDJSON + Merkle chain) | `everyaios-audit` | append-only events, seq + trace + payload digest; retention rolls payloads to `{rolled_up, payload_sha256}` |
| Session ledger | `everyaios-core` / `everyaios-blueprint` | task records, checkpoints, resume state |
| Memory | `everyaios-memory` | FTS5 semantic index, graph store, taste profiles, FSRS review queue |
| Catalog | `everyaios-catalog` | models.dev snapshot + provider profile overlays |
| Filename index | `everyaios-storage` | FTS5 filename index + notify-debouncer incremental updates |

### 1.7 The security model (Guard-1 vs Guard-2)

- **Guard-1** is *deterministic pre-exec scanning*: `prescan` (shell string, path, URL), `blocklist`
  (40+ patterns in 8 categories), `pathfloor` (canonicalize / symlink-safe / `..` prevention / fuzz gate),
  `netfloor` (the ONE network-destination classifier — cloud-metadata and link-local refused under every
  policy and not opt-in-able; RFC1918/CGNAT/ULA opt-in only; loopback default for local desktop but strict
  for untrusted-content URLs; decided on the *parsed* host so decimal/hex/octal/IPv4-mapped bypass forms
  normalize), `urlfloor`, `toctou` (re-verify at use time, closing the check-then-use gap), `structural`
  (shell-operator verdicts), `injection` (J6 `<user_document>` wrapping + estop), `loopguard` (SHA-256
  circuit breaker), `manifest` (Ed25519-signed extension manifests), `protected_paths` (other harnesses'
  config surfaces — `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, `.mcp.json`, `.env*`, `.ssh/`, `.aws/`, `id_rsa`,
  `id_ed25519`, `service_account.json` — are write floors while `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` stay editable).
- **Guard-2** is the *human-in-the-loop* gate: `permissions::PermissionsPolicy` parses
  `~/.everyaios/permissions.toml` into `Rule::{AlwaysAsk, AlwaysAllow, Block, AskIfGt, AskIfNewDomain,
  AskIfDestructive}`, `decision::DecisionPackage` carries goal/diff/risk/paths/network, `ticket` mints
  self-hashed receipts, `diffcard::render_native_card` draws the approval card.
- **Sandboxing** is fail-closed by default: `SandboxProfile` layers no_new_privs → per-path allowlist →
  syscall groups, with Renderer/Worker/Network roles; Linux gets a real `bwrap` launch with a postflight
  receipt; **the kernel-apply seam is unsupported on macOS and Windows** (documented, not implied).
- **Renderer isolation**: strict CSP in `tauri.conf.json`; `src-tauri/capabilities/*.json` grant only
  `core:*` window controls plus `dialog:allow-open` — no `fs`, `shell`, or `http` plugin privileges. The
  guard window loads only the bundled `guard.html`, and `guard_cmds::guard_respond` rejects any caller
  whose window label is not the guard window.

### 1.8 The verification pipeline

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs six jobs: `docs-sync` (capability index + TODO counts),
`rust` (fmt + clippy `-D warnings` + test on ubuntu/windows/macos), `office-oracle` (LibreOffice
round-trip conformance), `ui` (typecheck + build + bun test), `sidecar` (vendored-package check +
build + both test suites), `tauri-check` (cargo check/test on all three platforms). Alongside it:
`nightly-e2e.yml`, `p50-gates.yml`, `perf-regression.yml`, `release.yml`. Local gates are the four
`scripts/*.mjs` described in §7.

### 1.9 The headless executor node — the third topology (`deploy/`)

The topology above describes the desktop app and its sidecar. There is a **third way this system
runs**, and earlier revisions of this map did not cover it at all: the same `everyaios-core` binary in
the `--headless` profile, deployed as an always-on node on hardware **the user owns** — the P40
"BYO-host" pack (spec H33). It exists so scheduled/background work (B7) keeps running when the laptop
is shut.

**Contract.** The node is not a hosted service. `deploy/BYO-HOST.md` states three hard rules: no
founder account ever (every template provisions under the user's own login); **Guard-2 never leaves
the control plane** — approval-required steps park on the node as `pending` and surface on the
desktop; and the node's identity *is* the vault key (`EVERYAIOS_VAULT_KEY`) — env/secret only, never
inline, never committed. `src-tauri` is deliberately absent from the image, so a node can execute but
can never approve.

**Mechanism.** Six files, all in `deploy/` (§13.3 carries their parsed facts):

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `Dockerfile` | Three stages: `oven/bun:1` → `bun build --compile` the coordinator sidecar; `rust:1-slim` → `cargo build --release -p everyaios-core`; `debian:bookworm-slim` → runtime. |
| `docker-compose.yml` | One `everyaios-node` service; `EVERYAIOS_HOME=/data` volume; port **47615/tcp** (the P8.9 encrypted mesh transport); healthcheck `everyaios-core --version`; vault key from `.env` as `${EVERYAIOS_VAULT_KEY:?…}` — the stack *refuses to start* without it. |
| `fly.toml` | Fly.io template under the user's own account; `internal_port = 47615`; TCP health check; the comment directs you to Tailscale/private networking; `handlers = []` means no public handler by default. |
| `everyaios-node.service` | systemd: `ExecStart=…everyaios-core --headless --coordinator-bin /usr/local/bin/coordinator`, `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=5`, `EnvironmentFile=-/etc/everyaios-node.env`, dedicated `everyaios` user. |
| `com.everyaios.node.plist` | launchd agent, same argv, `RunAtLoad` + `KeepAlive{SuccessfulExit:false}`. |
| `BYO-HOST.md` | The pack's rules and install steps (indexed in §14). |

**Wiring — verified, not assumed.** Both flags are real and reachable: `--headless` at
`crates/everyaios-core/src/main.rs:49` (P40.1 profile comment), `--coordinator-bin` parsed by
`coordinator_bin_from_args` (`core/src/lib.rs:280`) and consumed at `main.rs:69` to start the
`ProcessSupervisor`. All three units pass both flags, which matters because `main.rs:63-65` warns
that **`--headless` without `--coordinator-bin` has no scheduler** — the argv is correct today, but
nothing in CI would catch it if it drifted (§7 item 13).

**Failure behaviour.** Compose restarts `unless-stopped` behind a 30 s / 3-retry `--version` health
check; systemd restarts on failure after 5 s; launchd restarts on unsuccessful exit; Fly gets a TCP
check on 47615. Note the health checks prove *liveness*, not *readiness* — `everyaios-core doctor`
(P46.2, spec H35, `main.rs:23-45`) exists and reports per-subsystem health, so a node that boots with
a broken vault currently still passes as healthy.

---

## 2. Low-level design — the core subsystems

Each subsystem below states **the contract, the mechanism, and the failure behaviour**, because in this
codebase the failure behaviour is usually the interesting part.

### 2.1 IPC & framing — `everyaios-ipc` ↔ `coordinator/{frame,message}`

`frame.rs` implements `[u32 LE length][JSON]`. `FrameDecoder::push` is incremental and partial-buffer
safe: a chunk may contain 0, 1, or N frames plus a partial tail, and a malformed length is a hard error
rather than a silent desync. The sidecar mirrors this in `coordinator/src/frame.ts`.

`message.rs` defines JSON-RPC 2.0 request/response/notification with `ERROR_CODES`. Both sides use
`PROTOCOL_VERSION = 1`; the `initialize` handshake negotiates it and advertises capabilities
(`streamDeltas`, `passByReference`) — **default-off in intent, currently on for both**, which is exactly
the kind of drift the version field exists to catch.

**Notification discipline matters:** `handleRequest` returns `null` for a notification and the loop
refuses to write a reply (`JSON-RPC 2.0 §2.2`). `session/heartbeat` and `agui/event` ride that path, as do
the retired `chat/cancel` · `plan/cancel` · `chat/provider_chunk` arms now that `P71.2c` removed their
senders. `mcp/search` answers **asynchronously** by writing the response itself and returning `null` — a
deliberate exception the code comments call out, because it is the easiest thing in the file to get wrong.

The live **shell→sidecar** surface is deliberately small after `P71.2c`: `initialize`, `echo`,
`chief/resolve` · `chief/set_session` · `chief/resolve_session`, `mcp/search`, `connector/list` ·
`connector/query`, `session/ping` · `session/shutdown`. `index.ts` carries an inline note naming exactly
which methods were retired and why, so a reader does not have to infer the absence.

### 2.2 Process supervision — `core::{supervisor,sidecar_link,orphan}`

`ProcessSupervisor` owns spawn/restart/backoff/circuit-break. It deliberately sets **no**
`BUN_JSC_heapSize` — Bun ≥1.3 rejects that env var and `exit(1)`s before running app code (a verified
bug, documented in `coordinator/src/index.ts`). Heap pressure is handled *in* the child by
`coordinator/src/heap.ts` (self-restart at 80%). `SidecarLink` re-arms the idle-watchdog clock on every
decoded frame. `orphan.rs` ensures a dead parent cannot leave a live sidecar.

### 2.3 The agent turn loop — **the bound agent's**, not ours

EveryAIOS owned a turn loop here (`coordinator/src/chat.ts`, ~1,558 lines: `runChatStream` →
`FrameProviderBridge` → `@everyaios/core-engine`'s `ConversationEngine`). **ADR-0005 §2 retired it** and
`P71.2c` archived the files to `ARCH/archive/coordinator-loop/` + `ARCH/archive/core-engine/`; `P71.2e`
archived the Rust `native_loop.rs` beside them. Kept only as history, its shape was: resolve the Chief →
assemble a 12-segment prompt → run the engine on a brokered provider stream → intercept tool calls (edit
ladder, CUA lanes, `dispatchSubAgent`) → record `ContextTrace`/`work-events`/observations → emit batched
deltas at 33 ms.

**What a v1 turn actually is** is §1.5 Flow 1: `acp_launch` → `acp_prompt` on the bound agent. The two
guarantees the deleted dispatch owned did not vanish — they moved onto that one path:

- **P71.3f readiness** — a turn runs only for a `Ready` agent, with the live ACP handshake as the
  evidence. A non-ready agent fails closed **with the state named**, never a generic engine error.
- **J11 session budget** — `ChatRelay::preflight_session_budget` refuses the turn before anything is
  dispatched once a session is at its `$` cap. A turn naming no session has no ledger to consult and is
  not gated.

EveryAIOS still files the *ledger* side of that turn: the readiness facts (`agent_readiness` /
`mount_readiness`), the execution record `acp_prompt` `begin`s, `work-events` → `work/event`, and
`recordObservation` → the routing ring. Observation is not inference — that distinction is what
ADR-0005 §4 keeps.

### 2.4 Prompt assembly — **archived** (`ARCH/archive/coordinator-loop/prompt.ts`)

> **Archived 2026-09-22 (`P71.2c`).** v1 compiles no prompt of its own: the bound agent owns its prompt,
its context and its cache boundary. The contract below survives as *identity* (`ARCH/13-PROMPT-ANATOMY.md`)
and as migration material for the post-v1 governed baseline binding (`P71.7`) — never as a v1 surface.

`buildDesktopSystemPrompt` emitted a **12-segment, cache-affine** prompt with an explicit `CACHE_BOUNDARY`.
Everything above the boundary is byte-stable across turns (identity, persona, rules, tool catalogue,
repo map); everything volatile (the current turn, retrieved documents, tool results) sits below it, so
the provider's prefix cache survives. `fitRepoMapToBudget` / `rankRepoMapTags` / `renderRepoMapBlock`
compress the repo map to a token budget using PageRank-ranked tags. `UserDocument` wraps untrusted
content in `<user_document>` (J6) — third-party retrieval is *data*, never instructions. The persona
system (`blueprint::persona::load_persona`, SOUL.md) emits `CORE_RULES` **first** and scans for
fail-closed overrides.

### 2.5 The tool registry & the edit ladder — `core/src/tools.rs`

`RegisteredTool` / `ToolRegistry` / `ToolService` define a 51-tool catalogue with a `ToolFamily` (`External`
is the third-party floor) and a `canonical_args_hash` used for ticket binding. MCP façade routing
(`FACADE_ROUTES`, `find_facade`, `is_facade`) lets one implementation serve both the native path and the MCP
`tools/list` path — the shared plane advertises façades only.

> The **coordinator-side half** of this section (`coordinator/src/tools.ts`: `INBUILT_TOOLS`,
`inbuilt_catalog`, the ~50-tool native catalogue handed to the built-in model) was archived with the loop
(`P71.2c`; catalogue narrowed at `P71.2f`). v1's tool surface for a turn is the bound agent's own tools plus
the shared plane; the Rust registry above remains the applier of record and the thing tickets bind to.

**The edit ladder** is three reachable rungs, each strictly more tolerant than the last:

| Rung | Matcher | Failure |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Exact | byte-exact `old` → `new` | fail closed |
| 2. Structured | **token-exact**: whitespace insignificant (an index map preserves positions), every other character exact | fail closed |
| 3. Fuzzy | normalized match | applies with the shape check *skipped* |

Two real defects were found and fixed here, and both are instructive: the structured rung used to call
`apply_exact_once` to locate its splice, so it could neither rescue nor refuse (it was dead code); and
`LexicalShapeSource`'s strip chain bound the *pre-strip* string, so a `pub fn` was restored **with** its
`pub ` prefix and matched no keyword — a pub-only file had an empty shape and passed the delta check
unconditionally. **The ladder's shape check is not a hard block**: a `ShapeChanged` refusal falls through
to the fuzzy rung, which applies the same splice without the check. That is a policy choice, pinned by a
test, not an oversight.

The ladder is **mirrored** coordinator-side on purpose: Rust's `file_ops.edit` commits, so it cannot hand
back the pre-commit post-state `execution/preflight` needs to inspect. Rust stays the applier of record.

### 2.6 Guard authorization — `guard::{prescan,ticket,permissions,decision,granter}`

`GuardService` (`core/src/guard_service.rs`) is the single funnel. `guard/evaluate` returns a
`GuardDecision` (allow / ask / block) with a `BlockExplanation` or `AskReason`; `guard/respond` consumes
the human answer. `granter::CapabilityGranter` computes the *effective* authority as manifest allow-list
∧ host grant, with explicit agent binding and deny-narrows-allow refinement into `GrantedCapabilities.denied`.
`TrustFlags` are fail-closed: a declared dangerous power still requires sandbox + approval.
`autonomy::AutonomyPolicy` maps a user-facing preset onto `RiskClass`, which is what decides whether a
given operation can ever auto-approve.

### 2.7 The vault & broker — `everyaios-vault`

Layers: `keyring` (key pools per provider, priority weights), `credential_broker` / `broker` (native HTTP
transports for the operations that are still ours — OAuth token use, catalogue refresh), `ledger`
(`token_usage` rows → `usage/recent`, which the coordinator hydrates into its routing ring at boot so
routing survives restarts), `egress` (per-destination policy), `session_budget` (the hard `$` cap), and
`auth_bridge`.

> **The broker stopped being a model gateway (`P71.2c`/`P71.2d`).** It no longer proxies inference: an
> external agent calls its provider itself. The budget cap is therefore enforced at the **turn boundary**,
> not mid-stream — `ChatRelay::preflight_session_budget` refuses a turn that would start over budget and
> surfaces `budget_exceeded`. `keyring::reveal_for_spawn` is what remains of credential movement: it reveals
> one key for exactly one purpose (building a spawned agent's environment) and never returns it over IPC.

**Failover is honest and narrow:** rotation happens on **429 only**, with exponential cooldown 5s→300s.
A 5xx does **not** rotate keys. That asymmetry is deliberate and tested.

### 2.8 ACP — `everyaios-acp`

`messages.rs` implements ACP v1 wire types (camelCase keys, snake_case discriminators, `PROTOCOL_VERSION`,
`StopReason`, `ToolKind`, `SessionUpdate`, `PermissionRequest`) rather than depending on an external SDK.
`frame.rs` does newline-delimited JSON-RPC. `client.rs` (`AcpSession`) drives
initialize → `session/new` → `session/prompt`, collecting `session/update` and answering
`session/request_permission` through an `FnMut` policy seam. **An `Ask` never auto-allows**: it mints a
ticket, waits for the host's `guard_respond`, and denies on reject/timeout.

`registry.rs` seeds a **46-agent catalogue** (official ACP `registry.json` + ollama launch + Zed's `/acp`
ecosystem) with `AuthMode` badges and `Distribution::{binary,npx,uvx}`. `registry_index.rs` parses the
typed registry and merges it with version-pinning and `-acp` alias canonicalization, policy allow-list →
allow/ask/block. `installer.rs` downloads (256 MB ceiling) → sha256-verifies → extracts to
`<data_dir>/agents/<id>/<version>/pkg`. **The install is Guard-2-ticketed**: request mints, commit
consumes. The remaining open seam is a live two-CLI side-by-side run with real agent binaries.

### 2.9 MCP — `everyaios-mcp`

`protocol.rs` + `server.rs` implement JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio and loopback HTTP, with TTL/ETag tool
lists, MRTR continuation, and a stateless Streamable-HTTP mode for MCP 2026-07-28 (legacy stateful HTTP
retained behind adapters). `npx.rs` resolves the launcher from system PATH then `EVERYAIOS_BUNDLED_NODE`,
validates the package against `trusted_npx_package`, and rejects shell escapes (`-c`, `--call`,
bash/sh) *and* any argument flag beginning with `-` or `_` before spawn. `manager.rs` handles lifecycle
with a subtle rule: **identity survives `Stop`, and the first `tools/call` does not undo an explicit
`Stop`**. The third-party floor (`ToolFamily::External`, `operation: "external_network"`, `risk: "high"`)
blocks auto-approval on read-named foreign tools — a stranger's tool name is never trusted as local read.

### 2.10 Office — `everyaios-office`

`xlsx` embeds **IronCalc 0.8.3 as a Rust library** (not a sidecar) for DAG recalculation; numeric claims
never bypass it. `docx` maintains an anchored block tree and patches ZIP parts byte-preservingly; `pptx`
edits text runs/slides/notes; `pdf` (lopdf) does form-fill, text-replace, re-author, redaction,
annotations; `citation.rs` renders CSL and inserts into docx. `atomic`/`rollback`/`xml`/`zip` provide the
transactional substrate. `conformance` is the LibreOffice oracle. **A real bug was fixed here:**
`DocxEngine::patch_block` wrote new part bytes but kept the tree/ranges built at `open`, so a
length-changing edit made the next `render_block` fail `BlockNotFound` and a second edit target stale XML;
`DocxEngine::refresh_tree()` is now called after every successful patch.

### 2.11 Browser & CUA — `browser`, `cdp`, `desktop`

`cdp` owns loopback-only discovery, per-target sessions, protocol types, and Electron app probing
(`Browser: Electron/…` detection stays behind the loopback guard). `browser` renders a11y snapshots with
minted refs `[ref=eN]` scoped to `(document_id, url)`, short-circuits on line-diff/URL-change, stitches
iframes, and supports a **slim snapshot mode** (`collapse_text` + `SLIM_DEPTH_CAP`, ≤40% of full-snapshot
tokens). `protocol.rs` parses native / Anthropic-CUA / OpenAI-CUA / UI-TARS action shapes into one
`ActKind`. `desktop` is the real OS operator (platform capture, A11y tree, input injection, OCR,
readiness) — fail-closed with `desktop session not attached` on a headless host rather than pretending.

### 2.12 Memory & context economy — `everyaios-memory`

Retrieval signals are **caller-supplied**; this crate owns the math: `bm25` (Okapi BM25), `fusion`
(weighted RRF + dedupe + smart snippets + per-type budget caps + chunk-min-size merge), `rerank`,
`actr` (activation/decay + importance floor + associative recall), `graph` (entity/episodic nodes, typed
edges, temporal edge-versioning, spreading activation, depth cap), `paging` (Letta-style 3-surface
core/archival/recall with overflow eviction), `compaction` (snip/soft/force ratios, safe split points,
summarize-fail-open, `prefix_dirty`, PRUNE_PROTECT, plus a turn-loop `CompactionCoordinator`), `janus`
(dedup + regex collapse + AST prune), `rtk` (per-command ls/ps/git/du compression, measured 60–90%),
`fsrs` (FSRS-6) + `reinforce` (review queue), `planner` (context planner with token budget and
precedence), `rtk`, `taste`, `cognee`, `lazy_graph`, `passport`, `journey`, `avoid`, `repair` (tool-JSON
repair), `cache` (result + semantic), `embedding` (int8/binary quantization).

### 2.13 Worktrees, swarm & governance — `core::{worktrees,governor,multirun}`, `blueprint`

`WorktreeManager` creates isolated task worktrees under `.everyaios/worktrees/task-<id>` with a serialized
`GitOperationQueue` and disk-headroom reservations, and exposes a three-file blackboard
(`BLACKBOARD_PLAN`, `BLACKBOARD_FINDINGS`, `BLACKBOARD_RECEIPTS`) with a `MAX_RECEIPT_BYTES` cap.
`ConcurrencyGovernor` enforces the hard limits (`P64_MAX_DEPTH=2`, `P64_MAX_CONCURRENT=6`,
`P64_MAX_TOTAL`) and `DEFAULT_DENY_TASK_TOOLS` / `DELEGATE_BLOCKED_TOOLS`. `multirun` fans one prompt
across up to 5 models in parallel worktrees with 3-way merge fusion — **but `applyEditBatch` still has no
live consumer**, so the multi-file arm of the preflight gate has no production input yet.

### 2.14 Audit & replay — `everyaios-audit`

`session_log` is append-only with idempotency classes; `merkle` is a SHA-256 hash chain whose `verify`
returns the *first bad row*; `repair` implements a 7-phase session recovery
(`validate_session` → `RecoveryAction`); `retention` compacts events older than N days into
`{rolled_up, payload_sha256}` digests with a `log.rollup` header at seq 0, written atomically via
tmp+rename, with a documented single-writer caveat. `replay`, `receipt`, and `cockpit` serve the UI.

### 2.15 The UI state machine — `ui/src/lib/store.ts` (3,019 lines)

One Zustand store holds sessions, messages, tool calls, streaming identity, view/rail layout, approval
queues, and UX metrics. Two invariants are load-bearing: **every stream carries its identity**
(`streamId` on the message, so a late terminal event cannot settle a newer turn in the same session),
and **the composer autonomy level is reconciled with Rust's GuardService preset at boot**
(`syncAutonomyFromRust`) so a stale localStorage value can never grant more authority than the kernel
would. `chat-event-routing.ts` maps wire events → store mutations and is the single place a turn's
lifecycle is interpreted.

### 2.16 The command surface — `src-tauri/src/commands.rs`

A single `generate_handler!` with **345 entries**, and a comment explaining *why* it must stay single:
`Builder::invoke_handler` **replaces** the handler rather than appending, so a second call would silently
keep only the last list. `tests/registration_sync.rs` guarantees the list stays complete as commands are
added, and `scripts/ipc-parity.mjs` cross-references registrations against `invoke()` call sites.

---

## 3. Schemas & dictionaries

### 3.1 The wire contract (sidecar ⇄ shell)

| Direction | Method | Kind | Payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| shell→sidecar | `initialize` | req | `{protocolVersion, clientName, capabilities}` → `{serverName, serverVersion, capabilities, status:"ready"}` |
| shell→sidecar | `chief/resolve` `chief/set_session` `chief/resolve_session` | req | Chief/agent resolution + session pinning |
| shell→sidecar | `mcp/search`, `connector/list`, `connector/query` | req | `mcp/search` is async-answered |
| shell→sidecar | `agui/event` | note | AG-UI envelope line |
| shell→sidecar | `session/ping` `session/heartbeat` `session/ready` `session/shutdown` | mixed | liveness |
| sidecar→shell | `chat/ttft` `chat/batch` `chat/done` `chat/error` `chat/cancelled` `chat/plan_start` `chat/plan_step` `chat/plan_interrupt` `chat/plan_done` | note | enriched with run identity; carries ACP-turn and automation-run status |
| sidecar→shell | `guard/evaluate` `guard/respond` `usage/recent` `scheduler/fire_webhook` | req | the gate + ledger + webhook-ingress surface |
| sidecar→shell | `work/event` | note | durable Work transitions |

**Retired at `P71.2c`** (which is why they are absent rather than unlisted): `chat/stream`,
`chat/cancel`, `chat/tool_retry`, `chat/provider_chunk`, `plan/execute`, `plan/respond`, `plan/cancel`,
`provider/stream` and `scheduler/execute`. A v1 turn is an ACP prompt (§1.5 Flow 1), so there is no
compiled prompt and no provider chunk to send.

Enrichment: `run-identity.ts::envelopeEvent` wraps an event with `{sessionId, workId?, executionId?}` so the
UI can attribute a late event correctly. After `P71.2c` the **turn** producer that called it is gone; the
module is kept for the **Work-event** plane and for `P71.9c`, which decides whether the ACP live updates
feed the `chat/*` receive plane or it is removed (`index.ts` carries the same note).

### 3.2 Tauri event names

`chat-event` (split/batch/done/error), `guard-event` (`{kind: minted|approved|rejected|expired, ticketId, batch}`),
`agui-event` (`{line}`), `model-download` (`{kind, id, repo, filename, phase, doneBytes, totalBytes}`).

### 3.3 Configuration & environment dictionaries

| File / var | Owner | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `everyaios.toml` | Rust core | ports, dirs, retention, vault path, browser resolution |
| `~/.everyaios/permissions.toml` | Guard | the user-owned rule policy (never model-owned) |
| `agents/*.md` | sidecar | per-agent blueprints (models, limits, tools, permission mode) |
| `providers.toml` | vault | provider → key pool → routing weights |
| `.everyaios/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` | blueprint | procedural memory |
| `.everyaios/worktrees/task-<id>` | core | isolated agent workspaces |
| `lsp-config.json` | codeintel | language-server launch config |
| `capabilities.yaml` | docs | the 166-row capability index (source of truth for `check-doc-sync`) |
| `EVERYAIOS_HOME` | core | data dir override (default `~/.everyaios`); on a node, `/data` or `/var/lib/everyaios` |
| `EVERYAIOS_VAULT_KEY` | vault | key override (tests) |
| `EVERYAIOS_VAULT_KEY` **(node)** | vault | on the executor node this is the node's **identity and P8.9 mesh key material** — supplied by secret / `EnvironmentFile`, never inline (§1.9) |
| port `47615/tcp` | node | the encrypted mesh transport an executor node exposes; keep it on a private network (§1.9) |
| `EVERYAIOS_HEARTBEAT_MS` | coordinator | heartbeat interval |
| `EVERYAIOS_BUNDLED_NODE` | mcp | node resolution fallback |
| `EVERYAIOS_LIVE_TEST` | tests | opt-in for live Chrome / LibreOffice / CDP suites |

### 3.4 Numeric constants worth knowing

`P64_MAX_DEPTH=2` · `P64_MAX_CONCURRENT=6` · `P64_MAX_TOTAL` · `P64_MAX_EDIT_BYTES` ·
`P64_MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES` (50 KB cap) · `MAX_RECEIPT_BYTES` · `MAX_ACTIVE_SKILLS=20` ·
`PREVIEW_BUDGET_TOKENS` · `CORE_BUDGET_TOKENS` · `DEFAULT_MAX_DEPTH` / `DEFAULT_TOP_K` (graph) ·
`FAILED_RECLAIM_AFTER` · `IDENTICAL_FAIL_HALT=2` · `CHIEF_SPEND_WARN` (>20%) ·
`DEFAULT_IMPORTANCE_FLOOR` · heartbeat 10s / idle watchdog 30s · stream batch 33 ms · catalog sync 4h ·
replay retention 7d · sandbox 64 MB heap / 512 KB stack / 30 s / 2 MB return · installer 256 MB ceiling ·
key cooldown 5s→300s · node mesh port 47615.

## 4. Dependencies

**Rust workspace** (`crates/Cargo.toml`): `serde`/`serde_json`, `thiserror`, `regex`,
`rusqlite` (bundled **SQLCipher**), `toml`, `ureq` (tls+json), `zeroize`, `rand 0.8`, `sha2`, `argon2`,
`sha1`, `base64`, `urlencoding`, `chacha20poly1305`, `x25519-dalek`, `tokio`
(rt/net/time/macros/sync), `tokio-tungstenite`, `futures-util`, `url`, `zip`, `similar`, `roxmltree`,
`flate2`, `crc32fast`, `tar`, `image` (png/jpeg), `opentelemetry`. Edition 2021, MSRV 1.80.
Notably **absent**: `tree-sitter` (repomap is lexical; tree-sitter is a pluggable `TagSource` upgrade)
and `hf-hub` (the HuggingFace client is hand-rolled over `ureq`).

**TS workspace** (`pnpm-workspace.yaml` globs `packages/*` + `ui`): the coordinator depends on
`@everyaios/core-ai`, `core-connectors`, `core-providers` and `core-search`, all `workspace:*` — the
`core-engine` dependency went with the loop (`P71.2c`). `allowBuilds` whitelists `better-sqlite3`, `esbuild`, `onnxruntime-node`, `workerd`.
React types are force-overridden to `^19` across the workspace so the UI program sees one React type
identity.

**Sidecar build:** `bun build --compile ./src/index.ts --external canvas --outfile dist/coordinator`,
copied to `src-tauri/bin/coordinator` (the `tauri.conf.json` resource glob hard-fails if missing).

**Per-crate and per-package dependency tables are generated in §12.**

| Command | Proves | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `cd crates && cargo test --workspace` | all Rust unit + integration tests | must run **from `crates/`** — the workspace manifest lives there, not at the repo root |
| `cd crates && cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` | lint-clean | CI runs with `-D warnings` |
| `cargo fmt --all -- --check` | formatting | |
| `cd ui && bun test` | UI unit tests | `pnpm run type-check` fails in a non-TTY shell (pnpm aborts an implicit modules purge); invoke `ui/node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit` instead |
| `ui/node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` | UI types | documented workaround above |
| `cd packages/coordinator && bun test` | sidecar tests | two `live-agent-harness` cases spawn the **real** `opencode` binary with no skip gate |
| `cd packages/coordinator && bun run type-check` | sidecar types | |
| `node scripts/check-doc-sync.mjs` | capabilities.yaml == ARCH/09 == spec §0, TODO header math | fast, no build |
| `node scripts/ipc-parity.mjs --md` | registered commands ↔ `invoke()` sites | prints ghost commands |
| `node scripts/clean-profile-boot-check.mjs` | honest locked/setup boot | no seeded demo data |
| `node scripts/e2e/security-gate.mjs` | 6 security legs | |
| `node scripts/e2e/failure-injection.mjs` | 6 failure legs | |
| `node scripts/verify-packaged-e2e.mjs` | packaged artifact boot | |
| `EVERYAIOS_LIVE_TEST=1 cargo test -p everyaios-browser` | real headless Chrome | needs Chrome |
| `EVERYAIOS_LIVE_TEST=1 cargo test -p everyaios-office conformance -- --ignored` | LibreOffice oracle | needs `libreoffice-writer` |

**Measured test corpus** (files, not cases): 11 crates have `tests/` dirs with 28 integration files
(core 9, acp 6, mcp 4, browser 2, and 1 each for blueprint/codeintel/desktop/memory/office/search/vault);
plus 54 UI test files, 59 coordinator test files, and 49 vendored `core-*` test files. Test **functions**
total 2,771 `#[test]` bodies in Rust (§9.0) and 162 test cases in TS/TSX by the §10 counter.

**This map is a build artifact and can be gated.** `node scripts/gen-codebase-map.mjs` regenerates
§9–§15; `--check` exits 1 when the inventory is stale, so it can join the `docs-sync` CI job alongside
`check-doc-sync.mjs`, `ipc-parity.mjs`, and `clean-profile-boot-check.mjs`. A plain run also performs
the §15.5 coverage audit and exits non-zero if any tracked file lacks an entry. The §9–§15 counts are
computed from `git ls-files` at generation time and cannot drift silently.

## 6. Wiring status — what is actually reachable

**Method.** For each Rust file, count references to its module name (`m::`) in every *other* source file
across `crates/` and `src-tauri/`. For each TS/TSX file, build the relative-import graph from the three
entry points (`ui/src/main.tsx`, `ui/src/guard-main.ts`, `packages/coordinator/src/index.ts`) and compute
reachability, separating files reachable only from tests. Full tables in §11.

**Caveats you must apply to §11:**
1. A module with a generic name (`types`, `config`, `state`, `util`) accumulates false-positive
   references; §11 flags these as `ambiguous` instead of claiming them wired.
2. Reference counting proves *reachability by name*, not *invocation on a live path*. A module can be
   referenced only from its own crate's `pub use` re-export list and still be dead at runtime.
3. TS reachability treats dynamic `import()` and string-keyed dispatch as edges only when they are
   statically visible.

**What the repo's own docs claim [C], vs what §11 measures [D]:**

| Claimed in docs [C] | Measured here [D] | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| ~48 Rust modules with zero references; all of `everyaios-engine` dead | 127 zero-reference files, of which the non-test source candidates are listed in §11.2 (`everyaios-engine`'s `gate`, `plan`, `risk` among them) | **Holds** — `everyaios-engine` still has no dependents in any `Cargo.toml` |
| ~21 coordinator modules imported only by tests | 32 test-only TS modules repo-wide (§11.5) | **Understated** |
| 60 "ghost" Tauri commands | 55 without a direct `invoke()` site — 10 indirectly referenced, **45 cold** (§11.6) | **Broadly holds** |
| 2,764 Rust `#[test]` fns | 2,771 (§9.0) | **Holds** |

Note also that the docs' "330 registered commands" figure is stale: `commands.rs` registers **339** today.

---

## 7. Known gaps, risks & contradictions

1. **Windows and macOS are unverified, and the docs now say so.** P68.7 (ConPTY/pwsh) and
   P57.6/P66.7 (Windows Graphics Capture + WinUia) were deliberately out of scope because no Windows
   host was available. The sandbox kernel-apply seam is explicitly **unsupported** on macOS/Windows.
   CI compiles and type-checks on all three platforms (that part is real); it does not *behave*-verify them.
2. **`desktop_app` is a phantom prefix.** `CURRENT_RUN.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, and
   `CODEBASE-MAP`-adjacent docs reference paths like `desktop_app/TODO.md`, but the repo *root is* that
   directory. CI works around it with a checkout `path: desktop_app`. Leftover from when this was a
   subfolder.
3. **Doc-vs-code drift is a known, recurring failure mode.** `capabilities.yaml` claims `everyaios-search`
   implements G1–G8, but the crate is a single module; `ARCH/02` lists features that live only in the
   coordinator. `check-doc-sync.mjs` catches *index* drift (ids and counts), not *capability* drift.
4. **`applyEditBatch` has no live consumer**, so the preflight gate's `filesChanged > 1` arm has no
   production input, and the "landed but failed" checkpoint badge state is unit-covered but unreachable
   in production today (the gate refuses failing writes before they land). Both are documented in the
   changelog rather than hidden.
5. **`everyaios-engine` is a fully dead crate** (`gate`, `plan`, `risk`) with no dependents — the
   permission/risk logic it holds was superseded by `everyaios-guard` + the coordinator mirror.
6. **Two coordinator tests require a real `opencode` binary** with no skip gate, so a clean CI machine
   can fail the sidecar job for environmental reasons.
7. **Timing-flaky test**: `p10_bench.rs::bench_browser_snapshot_tree_build` has a 500 ms budget and was
   observed at 587 ms once, then passed on re-run.
8. **`CURRENT_RUN.md` contradicts itself across entries** (e.g. 1,428 vs 1,429 TODO census; "330
   registered commands" vs the 349 in `commands.rs` today). Its own header says it is session handover
   only and must not carry a competing census. Treat its numbers as [C].
9. **`file_ops.replace` was a pinned tool id that no registry ever registered** — the kind of bug the
   `LOOP_PINNED_TOOL_IDS` list can produce when it drifts from `ToolRegistry`.
10. **The `failed` preflight badge is unreachable**, and `applyExactEdit` is now a misnomer (it rides the
    full ladder). Renamed only if a follow-up wants the churn.
11. **37 committed build artifacts.** `packages/*/.turbo/turbo-*.log` — turbo's per-package task logs, most
    under 1 KB, one 0 bytes — are tracked by git, and `.gitignore` has no `.turbo` rule at all (turbo
    would normally add one). They are recorded rather than quietly skipped: §13.6.
12. **Stale screenshot rules in `.gitignore`.** `*.png` is ignored as "Uploaded screenshots (not project
    assets)" with two `!` exceptions for root screenshots the comment says are *embedded in README*.
    README embeds neither — it references only `src-tauri/icons/128x128.png`. `5a1c3357-….png` is
    un-ignored but **absent from the tree** (`CURRENT_RUN.md:2202` records its deliberate removal), so
    that rule is dead; `8cfdc45d-….png` (52 KB) is tracked and named by nothing else in the repo. The
    comment and one of the two rules are stale.
13. **The deploy plane has no CI.** No workflow builds `deploy/Dockerfile`, runs the compose stack, or
    lints the systemd/launchd units; the pack is verified by its own comments only. Its argv *is* correct
    today (§1.9), but nothing would catch the one-line drift that leaves a node without a scheduler.

---

## 8. Coverage statement

**File coverage is now 1,416 / 1,416 — every tracked file has its own entry, and that is enforced.**
The generator registers the files each section covers and diffs that against `git ls-files`; a
non-empty diff exits non-zero. §15.5 prints the audit. The distribution:

| Section | Covers | Files |
|---|---|---:|
| §9 | Rust — per file, per item, per function | 504 |
| §10 | TS/TSX — per file, per export, per declaration | 623 |
| §12.2 | npm manifests | 13 |
| §13 | Everything else non-source: manifests, CI, the `deploy/` pack, configs, lockfiles, scripts, assets | 139 |
| §14 | Every `.md` — title, size, opening sentence | 137 |

What each tier claims:

- **§9–§15 are complete by construction** — enumerated from `git ls-files` at generation time, not
  maintained by hand. Every public Rust item and TS export is listed, every function (public *and*
  private) is named with its line number, and dependency/wiring tables are recomputed on each run.
- **§1–§7 are complete at the design level** for the 17 subsystems that carry correctness (§2.1–§2.16
  plus the deploy plane in §1.9). The remaining ~430 source files are enumerated and accounted for in
  §9–§11 but not individually narrated; for those, the file's own `//!` module doc (quoted in §9) is the
  best available description and the function list tells you where to read.
- **Documentation is accounted for, not summarised:** §14 gives all 137 `.md` files a title, line count,
  size, and opening sentence. The 104 `RESEARCH/` documents remain a literature corpus — they describe
  *other people's* repositories, and this map does not re-verify their claims.

An entry is accounting, not explanation. Where a file is listed in §13/§14 with one line of purpose
derived from its own header or parsed keys, treat that as [S]/[A] — enough to know what it is and that
it exists, not enough to reconstruct its behaviour.

---

## 10. TypeScript / TSX — per-area, per-file LLD

**Every tracked `.ts`/`.tsx` file is enumerated below** (619 of them). `exports:` lists every exported
declaration and re-export. `fns:` lists **every named function** — declarations, class and object-literal
methods, constructors, get/set accessors, and function/arrow expressions bound to a name — as `name:line`,
parsed with the TypeScript compiler. `anon:` counts the
function/arrow expressions with no binding name (inline callbacks); `fns + anon` is the file's full function
population. `wiring:` is the §11 verdict.

## 11. Wiring & status matrix [D]

### 11.1 Caveats — read these before trusting any table below

1. Reference counting proves **reachability by name**, not invocation on a live runtime path. A module
   referenced only by its own crate's `pub use` re-export block can still be dead at runtime.
2. Generic stems (`lib`, `types`, `config`, `state`, `error`, `helpers`, `prelude`, ...) match everywhere
   and are reported as `ambiguous` rather than counted.
3. Two modules with the same stem in different crates collapse into one reference bucket.
4. TS reachability treats only statically visible imports (`from`, bare `import`, `require`) as edges.
   Dynamic `import()` and string-keyed dispatch are missed.
5. Test files and Rust `tests/` fixtures are included in these tables on purpose (they are files too),
   which is why a mock server or an acceptance suite can appear as an "orphan".

- `ui/src/test/dom-harness.tsx`

### 11.6 Tauri commands with no static `invoke()` call site

340 commands registered. 285 have a direct `invoke("name")` site.
Of the 55 without one:

- **10 are only *indirectly* referenced** — the name appears as a string literal somewhere in the
  UI/coordinator, or Rust mentions it. These are probably fine (the UI may pass a variable, as
  `vault-gate.tsx` does with `invoke(cmd)`).
- **45 are cold** — the name appears nowhere outside its own definition and registration.

A high `Rust mentions` count on a generic word (`version`, `status`, `tasks`) is noise — the name
matches unrelated identifiers. Counts above 50 are flagged `(noisy)` and carry no signal.

### 13.3 The deployment plane (`deploy/`)

*Narrated in **§1.9** — the BYO-host pack that runs the same core binary headless on hardware the user owns. Five files here; the sixth, `BYO-HOST.md`, is a document and is indexed in §14.*

#### `deploy/Dockerfile` — 1.9 KB · 42 lines
- 3 build stages: `oven/bun:1 (coordinator)` → `rust:1-slim (core)` → `debian:bookworm-slim`
- `RUN` steps: 5 · base image is a slim Debian runtime; the coordinator is Bun-compiled, the core is `cargo build --release -p everyaios-core`
- runtime user/permissions hardening: none — container runs as root unless overridden

#### `deploy/com.everyaios.node.plist` — 1.4 KB · 42 lines
- launchd agent `com.everyaios.node` · args: `/usr/local/bin/everyaios-core --headless --coordinator-bin /usr/local/bin/coordinator /usr/local/var/everyaios /usr/local/var/log/everyaios-node.log /usr/local/var/log/everyaios-node.err.log`
- RunAtLoad `yes` · KeepAlive `yes`

#### `deploy/docker-compose.yml` — 1.3 KB · 43 lines
- services (3): `everyaios-node`, `everyaios-data`, `everyaios-mesh` · published ports: 1
- vault key is fail-fast (`${EVERYAIOS_VAULT_KEY:?…}`), so the stack refuses to start without it — never hardcoded

#### `deploy/everyaios-node.service` — 1.4 KB · 37 lines
- systemd unit `everyaios-node.service` · `ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/everyaios-core --headless --coordinator-bin /usr/local/bin/coordinator`
- User `everyaios` · restart `on-failure` · EnvironmentFile `/etc/everyaios-node.env`

#### `deploy/fly.toml` — 1.2 KB · 43 lines
- app `everyaios-node` · internal_port `47615` · health check `tcp`
